I've kicked off a survey of the codebase to gather exact conventions from app.py, runtime/server.py, runtime/session.py, and protocol/. I'll report back once that's done and then spawn the coder with a precise brief.

ok ask 7b528bcc $0.218 · 198,102 tok

what: Facts for spec'ing a new HTTP server module alongside the existing Unix-socket EngineServer.

verdict

All six items fully resolved with verbatim source. The codebase has no existing HTTP server, no logging module usage in server.py (print-only in app.py, ErrorOccurred events for protocol errors), and no request/response RPC pattern — command handling is fire-and-forget with fan-out event broadcast to all subscribers. A new HTTP module should decide: (a) reuse EngineSession.subscribe()/Subscriber for SSE/WebSocket push of events, and (b) reuse protocol.codec.encode/decode_command + protocol.commands/events dataclasses for JSON body shape, keeping the same NDJSON-style single-JSON-object convention (though HTTP may want single JSON objects per request/response rather than NDJSON framing).

facts
  1. app.py — full argparse setup, verbatim: ``pythondef parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace: parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Engine JSON-IPC server") parser.add_argument( "workspace", nargs="?", default=".", help="project root (default: current directory)", ) return parser.parse_args() ` No subcommands, no flags beyond default -h/--help (argparse auto-generated). Only one positional optional arg. Convention: lowercase help text, no trailing period, states default inline.

main() startup sequence, verbatim: `pythonasync def main() -> None: args = parse_args() workspace = Path(args.workspace).expanduser().resolve() engine_dir = workspace / ".engine" engine_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

print("=== Engine Server Startup ===", flush=True) print(f"workspace: {workspace}", flush=True) print(f"engine dir: {engine_dir}", flush=True) print(f"db path: {engine_dir / 'session.db'}", flush=True) print(f"socket path: {engine_dir / 'engine.sock'}", flush=True) print(f"python version: {sys.version.split()[0]}", flush=True) print(f"platform: {platform.platform()}", flush=True) print("==============================", flush=True)

session = EngineSession(workspace, db_path=engine_dir / "session.db") await session.start() server = EngineServer(session, socket_path=engine_dir / "engine.sock") loop = asyncio.get_running_loop() force = False

async def _graceful() -> None: await session.aclose() server.stop()

def _stop() -> None: nonlocal force if force: session.close_session() server.stop() return force = True loop.create_task(_graceful())

for sig in (signal.SIGINT, signal.SIGTERM): loop.add_signal_handler(sig, _stop)

print(f"listening on {engine_dir / 'engine.sock'}", flush=True) await server.serve()

if __name__ == "__main__": asyncio.run(main()) ` Key pattern for adding an HTTP server module: construct one EngineSession (shared), await session.start() once, then hand the same session to one or more transport servers (Unix EngineServer, new HttpServer), start signal handlers once, then run whichever server(s) via asyncio.gather or similar in main(). Currently only one server is started — a coder adding HTTP will need to modify main() to run both server.serve() and a new http server's serve loop concurrently (e.g. await asyncio.gather(server.serve(), http_server.serve())), and extend _graceful/_stop to also stop the http server.

  1. runtime/server.py — EngineServer, verbatim: `pythonclass EngineServer: def __init__(self, session: EngineSession, socket_path: Path): self._session = session self._socket_path = socket_path self._stopped: asyncio.Event | None = None

def _stop_event(self) -> asyncio.Event: if self._stopped is None: self._stopped = asyncio.Event() return self._stopped

async def serve(self) -> None: self._socket_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) if self._socket_path.exists(): self._socket_path.unlink()

server = await asyncio.start_unix_server( self._on_client, path=str(self._socket_path), limit=STREAM_LIMIT ) try: async with server: await self._stop_event().wait() finally: if self._socket_path.exists(): self._socket_path.unlink()

def stop(self) -> None: self._stop_event().set()

async def _on_client( self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter, ) -> None: queue = self._session.subscribe() read_task = asyncio.create_task(self._read_commands(reader)) write_task = asyncio.create_task(self._write_events(writer, queue)) try: done, pending = await asyncio.wait( {read_task, write_task}, return_when=asyncio.FIRST_COMPLETED, ) for task in pending: task.cancel() await asyncio.gather(*pending, return_exceptions=True) for task in done: if task.cancelled(): continue exc = task.exception() if exc is not None and not isinstance( exc, (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError) ): raise exc finally: self._session.unsubscribe(queue) writer.close() with suppress(OSError): await writer.wait_closed()

async def _read_commands(self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader) -> None: while True: line = await reader.readline() if not line: break try: command = decode_command(line) except (ProtocolError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc: self._session.emit_error(str(exc)) continue try: await self._session.handle(command) except Exception as exc: # noqa: BLE001 self._session.emit_error(f"{type(command).__name__} failed: {exc}")

async def _write_events( self, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter, queue: asyncio.Queue, ) -> None: while True: event = await queue.get() payload = encode(event) if len(payload) >= STREAM_LIMIT: payload = encode( ErrorOccurred( message=( f"dropped {type(event).__name__}: {len(payload)} bytes " f"exceeds the {STREAM_LIMIT} byte NDJSON line limit" ) ) ) writer.write(payload) await writer.drain() ` Constructor signature: EngineServer(session: EngineSession, socket_path: Path) — two positional/keyword args, no others. Transport is asyncio.start_unix_server; the connection handler is a plain callback _on_client(reader, writer) matching asyncio.start_unix_server's callback signature — for HTTP this would map to whatever your chosen framework's connection/request handler is. NDJSON framing: one JSON object per line, reader.readline() on read side, writer.write(encode(event)) + await writer.drain() on write side. Error handling style: decode/protocol errors -> session.emit_error(str(exc)) and continue (never crash the loop); command handler exceptions -> caught broadly, turned into ErrorOccurred event via emit_error; ConnectionError/BrokenPipeError from either task on join are swallowed, everything else re-raised. Logging style: no logging module used anywhere in this file — only errors get turned into protocol ErrorOccurred events sent to the client; app.py uses bare print(..., flush=True) for startup banner. There is no structured logger in this codebase's runtime/server layer — a new HTTP module should follow the same "no logger, emit ErrorOccurred / print for startup" convention unless told otherwise.

  1. runtime/session.py — EngineSession (lines 85-1329 total; core excerpt): `pythonclass EngineSession: def __init__(self, workspace: Path, db_path: Path): ... async def start(self) -> None: init_store(self._db_path)

async def handle(self, command: Command) -> None: fn = HANDLERS.get(type(command)) if fn is None: self._emit( ErrorOccurred(message=f"unknown command: {type(command).__name__}") ) return result = fn(self, command) if inspect.isawaitable(result): await result

def subscribe(self) -> Subscriber: queue = Subscriber( capacity=self._config.subscriber_capacity, max_bytes=self._config.subscriber_bytes, ) self._subscribers.append(queue) return queue

def unsubscribe(self, queue: Subscriber) -> None: try: self._subscribers.remove(queue) except ValueError: pass ... def emit_error(self, message: str) -> None: self._emit(ErrorOccurred(message=message)) ... def _emit(self, event: Event) -> None: if isinstance(event, ErrorOccurred) and self._metrics is not None: message = event.message or "" if not message.startswith("llm error:"): self._metrics.observe_error("session") for queue in list(self._subscribers): queue.put(event) `
- Constructor: EngineSession(workspace: Path, db_path: Path). Only two required positional args, no session-id/config args at construction (config, LLM, etc. all derived internally from workspace/env inside __init__).
- handle(command: Command) -> None (an async def, always returns None). It is not request/response — it dispatches to a handler in runtime.commands.HANDLERS dict (keyed by type(command)), and that handler pushes zero or more Events to subscribers via self._emit(event). So there is no "single event or list of events" return; events flow purely through the subscribe/queue mechanism, asynchronously and independently of the handle() call's completion.
- subscribe() -> Subscriber: not an async generator, not a plain asyncio.Queue — it's a custom Subscriber object (see runtime/subscriber.py) with .put(event) (sync) and async def get(self) -> Event / get_nowait(). Appends to self._subscribers: list[Subscriber].
- unsubscribe(queue: Subscriber) -> None: removes from self._subscribers list; swallows ValueError if already removed (idempotent).
- close_session() -> bool and async def aclose() -> None handle full session teardown (abort turns, stop LSP, mcp aclose, emit SessionEnded).

  1. protocol/codec.py, full file verbatim (53 lines): `pythonfrom __future__ import annotations

import jsonfrom typing import Any

from protocol.commands import COMMANDS, Commandfrom protocol.events import EVENTS, Event

# asyncio StreamReader defaults to 64KiB per line; snapshots can exceed that. STREAM_LIMIT = 8 * 1024 * 1024

class ProtocolError(ValueError): pass

def encode(msg: Command | Event) -> bytes: return (json.dumps(msg.to_json()) + "\n").encode()

def decode_command(line: str | bytes) -> Command: data = _parse(line) return _dispatch(COMMANDS, data, kind="command")

def decode_event(line: str | bytes) -> Event: data = _parse(line) return _dispatch(EVENTS, data, kind="event")

def _parse(line: str | bytes) -> dict[str, Any]: if isinstance(line, bytes): line = line.decode() text = line.strip() if not text: raise ProtocolError("empty line") try: data = json.loads(text) except json.JSONDecodeError as exc: raise ProtocolError(f"invalid JSON: {exc}") from exc if not isinstance(data, dict): raise ProtocolError("JSON payload must be an object") return data

def _dispatch(registry: dict[str, type], data: dict[str, Any], kind: str): type_name = data.get("type") if not type_name: raise ProtocolError(f"{kind} is missing a type field") cls = registry.get(type_name) if cls is None: raise ProtocolError(f"unknown {kind} type: {type_name}") return cls.from_json(data) ` ProtocolError subclasses ValueError — server code catches (ProtocolError, TypeError, ValueError) together since ProtocolError IS a ValueError.

  1. protocol/commands.py & protocol/events.py — base classes and examples. Base (in protocol/message.py, shared by both Command=ProtocolMessage and Event=ProtocolMessage aliases): `python @dataclassclass ProtocolMessage: def to_json(self) -> dict[str, Any]: payload: dict[str, Any] = {"type": type(self).__name__} for item in fields(self): value = getattr(self, item.name) if value is None: continue payload[item.name] = _encode(value) return payload

@classmethod def from_json(cls, data: dict[str, Any]): hints = _type_hints(cls) kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {} for item in fields(cls): if item.name not in data: continue kwargs[item.name] = _decode(data[item.name], hints.get(item.name, Any)) return cls(**kwargs) ` Command = ProtocolMessage and Event = ProtocolMessage — same base class, just semantic aliases; distinguished only by which registry (COMMANDS vs EVENTS) their @command/@event decorator populated. Command registration pattern (protocol/commands.py): `python COMMANDS: dict[str, type[ProtocolMessage]] = {}

def command(cls: type[ProtocolMessage]) -> type[ProtocolMessage]: COMMANDS[cls.__name__] = cls return cls

@command @dataclassclass StartSession(ProtocolMessage): workspace: str session_id: str | None = None

@command @dataclassclass SubmitUserMessage(ProtocolMessage): text: str

@command @dataclassclass Shutdown(ProtocolMessage): pass ` JSON shape example for SubmitUserMessage(text="hi") -> {"type": "SubmitUserMessage", "text": "hi"}. For Shutdown() -> {"type": "Shutdown"}. Event registration pattern (protocol/events.py): `python EVENTS: dict[str, type[ProtocolMessage]] = {}

def event(cls: type[ProtocolMessage]) -> type[ProtocolMessage]: EVENTS[cls.__name__] = cls return cls

@event @dataclassclass ChatMessageAdded(ProtocolMessage): id: str role: str text: str ts: str agent_id: str = ""

@event @dataclassclass ErrorOccurred(ProtocolMessage): message: str

@event @dataclassclass FileTreeUpdated(ProtocolMessage): file_tree: list[FileTreeNode] # nested dataclass, uses to_json()/from_json() recursively ` JSON shape: {"type": "ErrorOccurred", "message": "..."}. Nested dataclass fields (e.g. GitStateUpdated.git: GitState) are serialized via value.to_json() recursively (see _encode in protocol/message.py), and None fields are omitted entirely from output.

6. Existing tests for runtime/server.py:
- tests/test_server.py — sync-style tests wrapping async logic in local async def run(): ... + asyncio.run(run()). Fixtures: `python @pytest.fixturedef short_tmp_path(): """short path to avoid AF_UNIX 104-char sun_path limit on macOS""" tmpdir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=".e_") yield Path(tmpdir) import shutil shutil.rmtree(tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)

@pytest.fixturedef session(short_tmp_path): db = short_tmp_path / "session.db" ensure_schema(db) sess = EngineSession(short_tmp_path, db) return sess

async def _wait_socket(path: Path, timeout: float = 2.0) -> None: loop = asyncio.get_running_loop() deadline = loop.time() + timeout while loop.time() < deadline: if path.exists() and path.is_socket(): return await asyncio.sleep(0.01) pytest.fail(f"socket did not appear: {path}") ` Then e.g. test_server_creates_socket starts serve_task = asyncio.create_task(server.serve()), awaits _wait_socket, calls server.stop(), awaits serve_task, asserts socket file gone.
- tests/test_server_coverage.py — uses pytest.ini's asyncio_mode = auto (pytest-asyncio) to write plain async def test_...(session): functions directly (no asyncio.run wrapper), and mocks reader/writer with unittest.mock.AsyncMock to drive server._on_client(reader, writer) directly, e.g. reader.readline = AsyncMock(return_value=b"") for EOF, or side_effect=[b"invalid json\n", b""] for protocol-error paths. session fixture here is async and calls await sess.start().
- runtime/store/edits.py::ensure_schema(db_path) must be called before constructing EngineSession in tests (creates sqlite schema file) — both test files import and call it in their session fixture.
- pytest.ini has asyncio_mode = auto, timeout = 30, timeout_method = thread — a new tests/test_http_server.py can use plain async def test_... without explicit @pytest.mark.asyncio`.

paths touched
  • app.py (CLI entry, argparse, main())

  9|from runtime.session import EngineSession
 10|
 11|
 12|class EngineServer:
 13|    def __init__(self, session: EngineSession, socket_path: Path):
 14|        self._session = session
 15|        self._socket_path = socket_path
 16|        self._stopped: asyncio.Event | None = None
 17|
 18|    def _stop_event(self) -> asyncio.Event:
 19|        if self._stopped is None:
 20|            self._stopped = asyncio.Event()
 21|        return self._stopped
 22|
 23|    async def serve(self) -> None:
 24|        self._socket_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 25|        if self._socket_path.exists():
 26|            self._socket_path.unlink()
 27|
 28|        server = await asyncio.start_unix_server(
 29|            self._on_client, path=str(self._socket_path), limit=STREAM_LIMIT
 30|        )
 31|        try:
 32|            async with server:
 33|                await self._stop_event().wait()
 34|        finally:
 35|            if self._socket_path.exists():
 36|                self._socket_path.unlink()
 37|
 38|    def stop(self) -> None:
 39|        self._stop_event().set()
 40|
 41|    async def _on_client(
 42|        self,
 43|        reader: asyncio.StreamReader,
 44|        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,
 45|    ) -> None:
 46|        queue = self._session.subscribe()
 47|        read_task = asyncio.create_task(self._read_commands(reader))
 48|        write_task = asyncio.create_task(self._write_events(writer, queue))
 49|        try:
 50|            done, pending = await asyncio.wait(
 51|                {read_task, write_task},
 52|                return_when=asyncio.FIRST_COMPLETED,
 53|            )
 54|            for task in pending:
 55|                task.cancel()
 56|            await asyncio.gather(*pending, return_exceptions=True)
 57|            for task in done:
 58|                if task.cancelled():
 59|                    continue
 60|                exc = task.exception()
 61|                if exc is not None and not isinstance(
 62|                    exc, (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError)
 63|                ):
 64|                    raise exc
 65|        finally:
 66|            self._session.unsubscribe(queue)
 67|            writer.close()
 68|            with suppress(OSError):
 69|                await writer.wait_closed()
 70|
 71|    async def _read_commands(self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader) -> None:
 72|        while True:
 73|            line = await reader.readline()
 74|            if not line:
 75|                break
 76|            try:
 77|                command = decode_command(line)
 78|            except (ProtocolError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
 79|                self._session.emit_error(str(exc))
 80|                continue
 81|            try:
 82|                await self._session.handle(command)
 83|            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
 84|                self._session.emit_error(f"{type(command).__name__} failed: {exc}")
 85|
 86|    async def _write_events(
 87|        self,
 88|        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,
 89|        queue: asyncio.Queue,
 90|    ) -> None:
 91|        while True:
 92|            event = await queue.get()
 93|            payload = encode(event)
 94|            if len(payload) >= STREAM_LIMIT:
 95|                payload = encode(
 96|                    ErrorOccurred(
 97|                        message=(
 98|                            f"dropped {type(event).__name__}: {len(payload)} bytes "
 99|                            f"exceeds the {STREAM_LIMIT} byte NDJSON line limit"
100|                        )
101|                    )
102|                )
103|            writer.write(payload)
104|            awai

14|def parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace:
15|    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Engine JSON-IPC server")
16|    parser.add_argument(
17|        "workspace",
18|        nargs="?",
19|        default=".",
20|        help="project root (default: current directory)",
21|    )
22|    return parser.parse_args()
23|
24|
25|async def main() -> None:
26|    args = parse_args()
27|    workspace = Path(args.workspace).expanduser().resolve()
28|    engine_dir = workspace / ".engine"
29|    engine_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
30|
31|    print("=== Engine Server Startup ===", flush=True)
32|    print(f"workspace: {workspace}", flush=True)
33|    print(f"engine dir: {engine_dir}", flush=True)
34|    print(f"db path: {engine_dir / 'session.db'}", flush=True)
35|    print(f"socket path: {engine_dir / 'engine.sock'}", flush=True)
36|    print(f"python version: {sys.version.split()[0]}", flush=True)
37|    print(f"platform: {platform.platform()}", flush=True)
38|    print("==============================", flush=True)
39|
40|    session = EngineSession(workspace, db_path=engine_dir / "session.db")
41|    await session.start()
42|    server = EngineServer(session, socket_path=engine_dir / "engine.sock")
43|    loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
44|    force = False
45|
46|    async def _graceful() -> None:
47|        await session.aclose()
48|        server.stop()
49|
50|    def _stop() -> None:
51|        nonlocal force
52|        if force:
53|            session.close_session()
54|            server.stop()
55|            return
56|        force = True
57|        loop.create_task(_graceful())
58|
59|    for sig in (signal.SIGINT, signal.SIGTERM):
60|        loop.add_signal_handler(sig, _stop)
61|
62|    print(f"listening on {engine_dir / 'engine.sock'}", flush=True)
63|    await server.serve()
64|
65|
66|if __name__ == "__main__":
67|    asyncio.run(main())

protocol/codec.py  lines 1-53 of 53
 1|from __future__ import annotations
 2|
 3|import json
 4|from typing import Any
 5|
 6|from protocol.commands import COMMANDS, Command
 7|from protocol.events import EVENTS, Event
 8|
 9|# asyncio StreamReader defaults to 64KiB per line; snapshots can exceed that.
10|STREAM_LIMIT = 8 * 1024 * 1024
11|
12|
13|class ProtocolError(ValueError):
14|    pass
15|
16|
17|def encode(msg: Command | Event) -> bytes:
18|    return (json.dumps(msg.to_json()) + "\n").encode()
19|
20|
21|def decode_command(line: str | bytes) -> Command:
22|    data = _parse(line)
23|    return _dispatch(COMMANDS, data, kind="command")
24|
25|
26|def decode_event(line: str | bytes) -> Event:
27|    data = _parse(line)
28|    return _dispatch(EVENTS, data, kind="event")
29|
30|
31|def _parse(line: str | bytes) -> dict[str, Any]:
32|    if isinstance(line, bytes):
33|        line = line.decode()
34|    text = line.strip()
35|    if not text:
36|        raise ProtocolError("empty line")
37|    try:
38|        data = json.loads(text)
39|    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
40|        raise ProtocolError(f"invalid JSON: {exc}") from exc
41|    if not isinstance(data, dict):
42|        raise ProtocolError("JSON payload must be an object")
43|    return data
44|
45|
46|def _dispatch(registry: dict[str, type], data: dict[str, Any], kind: str):
47|    type_name = data.get("type")
48|    if not type_name:
49|        raise ProtocolError(f"{kind} is missing a type field")
50|    cls = registry.get(type_name)
51|    if cls is None:
52|        raise ProtocolError(f"unknown {kind} type: {type_name}")
53|    return cls.from_json(data)

119|    def put(self, event: Event) -> None:
120|        size = approx_size(event)
121|        droppable = isinstance(event, DROPPABLE)
122|        if self._over_ceiling(0) and droppable:
123|            self.dropped += 1
124|            return
125|        if self._over_ceiling(size) and not droppable:
126|            self._evict_droppable(size)
127|        if self._over_ceiling(size) and droppable:
128|            self.dropped += 1
129|            return
130|        if len(self._items) >= self._capacity and not droppable:
131|            if not self._evict_droppable(size):
132|                self._collapse_overflow()
133|                return

 1|from __future__ import annotations
 2|
 3|from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
 4|from typing import Any, Union, get_args, get_origin, get_type_hints
 5|
 6|
 7|@dataclass
 8|class ProtocolMessage:
 9|    def to_json(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
10|        payload: dict[str, Any] = {"type": type(self).__name__}
11|        for item in fields(self):
12|            value = getattr(self, item.name)
13|            if value is None:
14|                continue
15|            payload[item.name] = _encode(value)
16|        return payload
17|
18|    @classmethod
19|    def from_json(cls, data: dict[str, Any]):
20|        hints = _type_hints(cls)
21|        kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {}
22|        for item in fields(cls):
23|            if item.name not in data:
24|                continue
25|            kwargs[item.name] = _decode(data[item.name], hints.get(item.name, Any))
26|        return cls(**kwargs)
27|
28|
29|def _type_hints(cls) -> dict[str, Any]:
30|    try:
31|        return get_type_hints(cls)
32|    except TypeError:
33|        module = __import__(cls.__module__, fromlist=["*"])
34|        globalns = vars(module)
35|        hints: dict[str, Any] = {}
36|        for name, raw in getattr(cls, "__annotations__", {}).items():
37|            hints[name] = _resolve_annotation(raw, globalns)
38|        return hints
39|
40|
41|def _resolve_annotation(raw: Any, globalns: dict[str, Any]) -> Any:
42|    if not isinstance(raw, str):
43|        return raw
44|    text = raw.strip()
45|    optional = False
46|    if text.endswith("| None"):
47|        text = text[: -len("| None")].rstrip()
48|        optional = True
49|    elif text.endswith("|None"):
50|        text = text[: -len("|None")].rstrip()
51|        optional = True
52|    try:
53|        resolved = eval(text, {"__builtins__": __builtins__}, globalns)
54|    except Exception:
55|        resolved = Any
56|    if optional:
57|        return Union[resolved, type(None)]
58|    return resolved
59|
60|
61|def _encode(value: Any) -> Any:
62|    if hasattr(value, "to_json") and callable(value.to_json):
63|        return value.to_json()
64|    if isinstance(value, list):
65|        return [_encode(item) for item in value]
66|    return value
67|
68|
69|def _decode(value: Any, annotation: Any) -> Any:
70|    annotation, optional = _unwrap_optional(annotation)
71|    if optional and (value is None or value == ""):
72|        return None
73|    origin = get_origin(annotation)
74|    if origin is list:
75|        inner = get_args(annotation)[0] if get_args(annotation) else Any
76|        return [_decode(item, inner) for item in value or []]
77|    if isinstance(annotation, type) and hasattr(annotation, "from_json"):
78|        if isinstance(value, annotation):
79|            return value
80|        return annotation.from_json(value)
81|    return value
82|
83|
84|def _unwrap_optional(annotation: Any) -> tuple[Any, bool]:
85|    if get_origin(annotation) is Union:
86|        args = [arg for arg in get_args(annotation) if arg is not type(None)]
87|        if len(args) == 1:
88|            return args[0], True
89|    return annotation, False

 14|async def _wait_socket(path: Path, timeout: float = 2.0) -> None:
 15|    loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
 16|    deadline = loop.time() + timeout
 17|    while loop.time() < deadline:
 18|        if path.exists() and path.is_socket():
 19|            return
 20|        await asyncio.sleep(0.01)
 21|    pytest.fail(f"socket did not appear: {path}")

 34|def short_tmp_path():
 35|    """Create a temporary directory with a short path to avoid AF_UNIX length limits on macOS."""
 36|    # macOS AF_UNIX sockets have a 104 character sun_path limit
 37|    # pytest's tmp_path can create very deep paths that exceed this
 38|    # Use tempfile.mkdtemp with a short prefix instead
 39|    tmpdir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=".e_")
 40|    yield Path(tmpdir)
 41|    # Cleanup
 42|    import shutil
 43|    shutil.rmtree(tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)

 200|    def close_session(self) -> bool:
 201|        if self._state.session_id is None:
 202|            return False
 203|        self.abort_turn()
 204|        if isinstance(self._loop, Orchestrator):
 205|            self._loop.abort_all_children()
 206|            self._prompts.cancel_all()
 207|            self._kill_live_procs()
 208|        self._cancel_history_replay()
 209|        self._state.ended = True
 210|        self._persist()
 211|        self._emit(SessionEnded(reason="shutdown"))
 212|        self.detach_metrics()
 213|        self._state = SessionState()
 214|        self._loop = None
 215|        self._pending_user = []
 216|        self._inbox = []
 217|        self._stop_lsp()
 218|        mcp = self._mcp
 219|        self._mcp = None
 220|        if mcp is not None:
 221|            with suppress(RuntimeError):
 222|                loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
 223|                loop.create_task(mcp.aclose())
 224|        return True

verdict

Full suite python3 -m pytest tests/ → 1237 passed, 1 failed (tests/test_session_trace.py::test_on_tool_is_a_noop_when_tracing_disabled), confirmed pre-existing on a clean git stash of this diff (unrelated env/config-pollution flake, not caused by this change). get_diagnostics clean on runtime/http_server.py after fixing a pyright Task type-union nit in _handle_events; app.py/tests/test_http_server.py still show a stale pyright "Import could not be resolved" for runtime.http_server even though python3 -c "import runtime.http_server" succeeds — cosmetic/cache lag, not a real error.

facts
  • runtime/session.py::EngineSession.handle() is fire-and-forget (returns None); events arrive via async fan-out to subscribers, so /command subscribes before await session.handle(...) and drains only synchronously-available events afterward via get_nowait()/asyncio.QueueEmpty (confirmed exact empty-exception in runtime/subscriber.py get_nowait).
  • protocol.codec.decode_command raises ProtocolError (subclass of ValueError) for empty/invalid-JSON/non-dict/missing-type/unknown-type; mapped to HTTP 400 {"error": str(exc)}.
  • Did not touch protocol/, runtime/server.py, runtime/session.py, runtime/subscriber.py, or any other file — diff scoped exactly to app.py, runtime/http_server.py, tests/test_http_server.py as required.
paths touched
  • app.py (edited): added --http [HOST:]PORT argparse flag, _parse_http_addr helper, conditional construction of HttpServer, wiring into _graceful()/_stop() for clean shutdown, and asyncio.gather(server.serve(), http_server.serve()) only when --http is set (unchanged single-server path when omitted).
  • runtime/http_server.py (new): HttpServer class mirroring EngineServer's shape (_stop_event, serve, stop, _on_client). Hand-rolled HTTP/1.1 request-line + header + Content-Length body parsing. Routes: POST /command (decode via protocol.codec.decode_command, 400 on ProtocolError/ValueError/TypeError/UnicodeDecodeError; else subscribe → await session.handle(command) → drain via queue.get_nowait()/asyncio.QueueEmpty → unsubscribe → 200 with JSON array of event.to_json(); handler exceptions become session.emit_error(...), never crash/500), GET /events (SSE headers, session.subscribe(), loop await queue.get() writing data: <json>\n\n, races against reader EOF to detect client disconnect and clean up). Any other route/method → 404. No logging module used, only stdlib.
  • tests/test_http_server.py (new): 3 async tests using raw asyncio.open_connection (no aiohttp/httpx/requests) against HttpServer(session, host="127.0.0.1", port=0): POST /command happy path (ListSessions → SessionList event, 200 + JSON array), POST /command 400 on malformed JSON and on valid-JSON-unknown-type, GET /events SSE stream receiving a data: line after session.emit_error(...).
leftover

none within scope. The test_session_trace.py failure is pre-existing and out of scope (not part of the requested file set).

 12|class EngineServer:
 13|    def __init__(self, session: EngineSession, socket_path: Path):
 14|        self._session = session
 15|        self._socket_path = socket_path
 16|        self._stopped: asyncio.Event | None = None
 17|
 18|    def _stop_event(self) -> asyncio.Event:
 19|        if self._stopped is None:
 20|            self._stopped = asyncio.Event()
 21|        return self._stopped
 22|
 23|    async def serve(self) -> None:
 24|        self._socket_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 25|        if self._socket_path.exists():
 26|            self._socket_path.unlink()
 27|
 28|        server = await asyncio.start_unix_server(
 29|            self._on_client, path=str(self._socket_path), limit=STREAM_LIMIT
 30|        )
 31|        try:
 32|            async with server:
 33|                await self._stop_event().wait()
 34|        finally:
 35|            if self._socket_path.exists():
 36|                self._socket_path.unlink()
 37|
 38|    def stop(self) -> None:
 39|        self._stop_event().set()
 40|
 41|    async def _on_client(
 42|        self,
 43|        reader: asyncio.StreamReader,
 44|        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,
 45|    ) -> None:
 46|        queue = self._session.subscribe()
 47|        read_task = asyncio.create_task(self._read_commands(reader))
 48|        write_task = asyncio.create_task(self._write_events(writer, queue))
 49|        try:
 50|            done, pending = await asyncio.wait(
 51|                {read_task, write_task},
 52|                return_when=asyncio.FIRST_COMPLETED,
 53|            )
 54|            for task in pending:
 55|                task.cancel()
 56|            await asyncio.gather(*pending, return_exceptions=True)
 57|            for task in done:
 58|                if task.cancelled():
 59|                    continue
 60|                exc = task.exception()
 61|                if exc is not None and not isinstance(
 62|                    exc, (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError)
 63|                ):
 64|                    raise exc
 65|        finally:
 66|            self._session.unsubscribe(queue)
 67|            writer.close()
 68|            with suppress(OSError):
 69|                await writer.wait_closed()
 70|
 71|    async def _read_commands(self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader) -> None:
 72|        while True:
 73|            line = await reader.readline()
 74|            if not line:
 75|                break
 76|            try:
 77|                command = decode_command(line)
 78|            except (ProtocolError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
 79|                self._session.emit_error(str(exc))
 80|                continue
 81|            try:
 82|                await self._session.handle(command)
 83|            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
 84|                self._session.emit_error(f"{type(command).__name__} failed: {exc}")
 85|
 86|    async def _write_events(
 87|        self,
 88|        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,
 89|        queue: asyncio.Queue,
 90|    ) -> None:
 91|        while True:
 92|            event = await queue.get()
 93|            payload = encode(event)
 94|            if len(payload) >= STREAM_LIMIT:
 95|                payload = encode(
 96|                    ErrorOccurred(
 97|                        message=(
 98|                            f"dropped {type(event).__name__}: {len(payload)} bytes "
 99|                            f"exceeds the {STREAM_LIMIT} byte NDJSON line limit"
100|                        )
101|                    )
102|                )
103|            writer.write(payload)
104|            awai

116|        self._overflowed = False
117|        self.dropped = 0
118|
119|    def put(self, event: Event) -> None:
120|        size = approx_size(event)
121|        droppable = isinstance(event, DROPPABLE)
122|        if self._over_ceiling(0) and droppable:
123|            self.dropped += 1
124|            return
125|        if self._over_ceiling(size) and not droppable:
126|            self._evict_droppable(size)
127|        if self._over_ceiling(size) and droppable:
128|            self.dropped += 1
129|            return
130|        if len(self._items) >= self._capacity and not droppable:
131|            if not self._evict_droppable(size):
132|                self._collapse_overflow()
133|                return
134|        self._items.append(event)
135|        self._bytes += size

protocol/codec.py  lines 1-53 of 53
 1|from __future__ import annotations
 2|
 3|import json
 4|from typing import Any
 5|
 6|from protocol.commands import COMMANDS, Command
 7|from protocol.events import EVENTS, Event
 8|
 9|# asyncio StreamReader defaults to 64KiB per line; snapshots can exceed that.
10|STREAM_LIMIT = 8 * 1024 * 1024
11|
12|
13|class ProtocolError(ValueError):
14|    pass
15|
16|
17|def encode(msg: Command | Event) -> bytes:
18|    return (json.dumps(msg.to_json()) + "\n").encode()
19|
20|
21|def decode_command(line: str | bytes) -> Command:
22|    data = _parse(line)
23|    return _dispatch(COMMANDS, data, kind="command")
24|
25|
26|def decode_event(line: str | bytes) -> Event:
27|    data = _parse(line)
28|    return _dispatch(EVENTS, data, kind="event")
29|
30|
31|def _parse(line: str | bytes) -> dict[str, Any]:
32|    if isinstance(line, bytes):
33|        line = line.decode()
34|    text = line.strip()
35|    if not text:
36|        raise ProtocolError("empty line")
37|    try:
38|        data = json.loads(text)
39|    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
40|        raise ProtocolError(f"invalid JSON: {exc}") from exc
41|    if not isinstance(data, dict):
42|        raise ProtocolError("JSON payload must be an object")
43|    return data
44|
45|
46|def _dispatch(registry: dict[str, type], data: dict[str, Any], kind: str):
47|    type_name = data.get("type")
48|    if not type_name:
49|        raise ProtocolError(f"{kind} is missing a type field")
50|    cls = registry.get(type_name)
51|    if cls is None:
52|        raise ProtocolError(f"unknown {kind} type: {type_name}")
53|    return cls.from_json(data)

 14|async def _wait_socket(path: Path, timeout: float = 2.0) -> None:
 15|    loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
 16|    deadline = loop.time() + timeout
 17|    while loop.time() < deadline:
 18|        if path.exists() and path.is_socket():
 19|            return
 20|        await asyncio.sleep(0.01)
 21|    pytest.fail(f"socket did not appear: {path}")
 22|
 23|
 24|@pytest.fixture
 25|def session(short_tmp_path):
 26|    """Create a test EngineSession."""
 27|    db = short_tmp_path / "session.db"
 28|    ensure_schema(db)
 29|    sess = EngineSession(short_tmp_path, db)
 30|    return sess
 31|
 32|
 33|@pytest.fixture
 34|def short_tmp_path():
 35|    """Create a temporary directory with a short path to avoid AF_UNIX length limits on macOS."""
 36|    # macOS AF_UNIX sockets have a 104 character sun_path limit
 37|    # pytest's tmp_path can create very deep paths that exceed this
 38|    # Use tempfile.mkdtemp with a short prefix instead
 39|    tmpdir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=".e_")
 40|    yield Path(tmpdir)
 41|    # Cleanup
 42|    import shutil
 43|    shutil.rmtree(tmpdir, ignore_errors=True)
 44|
 45|
 46|def test_server_creates_socket(session, short_tmp_path):
 47|    """Test that EngineServer creates a Unix domain socket."""
 48|
 49|    async def run():
 50|        socket_path = short_tmp_path / ".engine" / "engine.sock"
 51|        server = EngineServer(session, socket_path)
 52|
 53|        # Start server in background
 54|        serve_task = asyncio.create_task(server.serve())
 55|        await _wait_socket(socket_path)
 56|
 57|        # Stop server
 58|        server.stop()
 59|        await serve_task
 60|
 61|        # Socket should be cleaned up
 62|        assert not socket_path.exists()
 63|
 64|    asyncio.run(run())
 65|

 32|@handles(StartSession)
 33|async def start_session(session, command: StartSession) -> None:
 34|    incoming = Path(command.workspace).expanduser().resolve()
 35|    if incoming != session._workspace:
 36|        session._emit(
 37|            ErrorOccurred(
 38|                message=(
 39|                    f"workspace mismatch: got {incoming}, "
 40|                    f"expected {session._workspace}"
 41|                )
 42|            )
 43|        )
 44|        return
 45|
 46|    session._persist()
 47|    if command.session_id:
 48|        loaded = load_snapshot(session._db_path, command.session_id)
 49|        if loaded is None:
 50|            session._emit(
 51|                ErrorOccurred(message=f"unknown session: {command.session_id}")
 52|            )
 53|            return
 54|        session.detach_metrics()
 55|        session._state = SessionState.from_snapshot(loaded)
 56|    else:
 57|        session.detach_metrics()
 58|        session._state = SessionState(session_id=uuid4().hex)
 59|        session._persist()
 60|    await session._bind_loop()
 61|    session.attach_metrics()
 62|    session._emit_snapshot()
 63|    if session.language.warning:
 64|        session._emit(WarningOccurred(message=session.language.warning))

 74|    if not session._require_session():

 77|    if pending is not None:
 78|        if is_settle_prompt(pending.choices):

 80|            if intent is None:

 85|                return

 87|            return

+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import asyncio
+import json
+from contextlib import suppress
+
+from protocol.codec import ProtocolError, decode_command
+from runtime.session import EngineSession
+
+_MAX_HEADER_BYTES = 64 * 1024
+
+
+class HttpServer:
+    def __init__(self, session: EngineSession, host: str, port: int):
+        self._session = session
+        self._host = host
+        self._port = port
+        self._stopped: asyncio.Event | None = None
+        self._server: asyncio.base_events.Server | None = None
+
+    def _stop_event(self) -> asyncio.Event:
+        if self._stopped is None:
+            self._stopped = asyncio.Event()
+        return self._stopped
+
+    async def serve(self) -> None:
+        server = await asyncio.start_server(
+            self._on_client, host=self._host, port=self._port
+        )
+        self._server = server
+        try:
+            async with server:
+                await self._stop_event().wait()
+        finally:
+            self._server = None
+
+    def stop(self) -> None:
+        self._stop_event().set()
+
+    async def _on_client(
+        self,
+        reader: asyncio.StreamReader,
+        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,
+    ) -> None:
+        try:
+            request = await self._read_request(reader)
+            if request is None:
+                return
+            method, path, body = request
+            if method == "POST" and path == "/command":
+                await self._handle_command(writer, body)
+            elif method == "GET" and path == "/events":
+                await self._handle_events(reader, writer)
+            else:
+                await self._write_response(writer, 404, {"error": "not found"})
+        except (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError):
+            pass
+        finally:
+            writer.close()
+            with suppress(OSError):
+                await writer.wait_closed()
+
+    async def _read_request(
+        self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader
+    ) -> tuple[str, str, bytes] | None:
+        request_line = await reader.readline()
+        if not request_line:
+            return None
+        parts = request_line.decode("latin-1").strip().split()
+        if len(parts) < 2:
+            return None
+        method, path = parts[0], parts[1]
+
+        headers: dict[str, str] = {}
+        total = len(request_line)
+        while True:
+            line = await reader.readline()
+            total += len(line)
+            if total > _MAX_HEADER_BYTES:
+                return None
+            if not line or line in (b"\r\n", b"\n"):
+                break
+            text = line.decode("latin-1").strip()
+            if ":" in text:
+                key, _, value = text.partition(":")
+                headers[key.strip().lower()] = value.strip()
+
+        body = b""
+        if method == "POST":
+            length = int(headers.get("content-length", "0") or "0")
+            if length > 0:
+                body = await reader.readexactly(length)
+        return method, path, body
+
+    async def _handle_command(
+        self, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter, body: bytes
+    ) -> None:
+        try:
+            text = body.decode("utf-8")
+            command = decode_command(text)
+        except (ProtocolError, ValueError, TypeError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
+            await self._write_response(writer, 400, {"error": str(exc)})
+            return
+
+        queue = self._session.subscribe()
+        events = []
+        try:
+            await self._session.handle(command)
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
+            self._session.emit_error(f"{type(command).__name__} failed: {exc}")
+        finally:
+            while True:
+                try:
+                    events.append(queue.get_nowait())
+                except asyncio.QueueEmpty:
+

+def _parse_http_addr(value: str) -> tuple[str, int]:
+    if ":" in value:
+        host, _, port = value.rpartition(":")
+        return host, int(port)
+    return "127.0.0.1", int(value)

+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import asyncio
+import json
+
+import pytest
+
+from runtime.http_server import HttpServer
+from runtime.session import EngineSession
+from runtime.store.edits import ensure_schema
+
+
+@pytest.fixture
+async def session(tmp_path):
+    db = tmp_path / "session.db"
+    ensure_schema(db)
+    sess = EngineSession(tmp_path, db)
+    await sess.start()
+    return sess
+
+
+async def _start_http_server(session) -> tuple[HttpServer, asyncio.Task, int]:
+    server = HttpServer(session, host="127.0.0.1", port=0)
+    task = asyncio.create_task(server.serve())
+    deadline = asyncio.get_running_loop().time() + 2.0
+    while server._server is None:
+        if asyncio.get_running_loop().time() > deadline:
+            raise TimeoutError("http server did not start")
+        await asyncio.sleep(0.01)
+    port = server._server.sockets[0].getsockname()[1]
+    return server, task, port
+
+
+async def _stop_http_server(server: HttpServer, task: asyncio.Task) -> None:
+    server.stop()
+    await asyncio.wait_for(task, timeout=2.0)
+
+
+async def _http_request(port: int, method: str, path: str, body: bytes = b"") -> tuple[int, dict, bytes]:
+    reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", port)
+    try:
+        request = f"{method} {path} HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: 127.0.0.1\r\n"
+        if body:
+            request += f"Content-Length: {len(body)}\r\n"
+        request += "\r\n"
+        writer.write(request.encode("latin-1") + body)
+        await writer.drain()
+
+        status_line = await reader.readline()
+        status = int(status_line.decode("latin-1").split()[1])
+
+        headers: dict[str, str] = {}
+        while True:
+            line = await reader.readline()
+            if not line or line in (b"\r\n", b"\n"):
+                break
+            text = line.decode("latin-1").strip()
+            if ":" in text:
+                key, _, value = text.partition(":")
+                headers[key.strip().lower()] = value.strip()
+
+        length = int(headers.get("content-length", "0") or "0")
+        response_body = await reader.readexactly(length) if length else b""
+        return status, headers, response_body
+    finally:
+        writer.close()
+
+
+async def test_post_command_happy_path(session):
+    server, task, port = await _start_http_server(session)
+    try:
+        body = json.dumps({"type": "ListSessions"}).encode("utf-8")
+        status, headers, resp_body = await _http_request(port, "POST", "/command", body)
+        assert status == 200
+        events = json.loads(resp_body.decode("utf-8"))
+        assert isinstance(events, list)
+        assert len(events) >= 1
+        assert events[0]["type"] == "SessionList"
+    finally:
+        await _stop_http_server(server, task)
+
+
+async def test_post_command_malformed_and_unknown_type(session):
+    server, task, port = await _start_http_server(session)
+    try:
+        status, _, resp_body = await _http_request(
+            port, "POST", "/command", b"not json"
+        )
+        assert status == 400
+        payload = json.loads(resp_body.decode("utf-8"))
+        assert "error" in payload
+
+        status, _, resp_body = await _http_request(
+            port,
+            "POST",
+            "/command",
+            json.dumps({"type": "NotARealCommand"}).encode("utf-8"),
+        )
+        assert status == 400
+        payload = json.loads(resp_body.decode("utf-8"))
+        assert "error" in payload
+    finally:
+        await _stop_http_server(server, task)
+

 23|            self._stopped = asyncio.Event()
 24|        return self._stopped
 25|
 26|    async def serve(self) -> None:
 27|        server = await asyncio.start_server(
 28|            self._on_client, host=self._host, port=self._port
 29|        )
 30|        self._server = server
 31|        try:
 32|            async with server:
 33|                await self._stop_event().wait()
 34|        finally:
 35|            self._server = None
 36|
 37|    def stop(self) -> None:
 38|        self._stop_event().set()
 39|
 40|    async def _on_client(
 41|        self,
 42|        reader: asyncio.StreamReader,
 43|        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,
 44|    ) -> None:
 45|        try:
 46|            request = await self._read_request(reader)
 47|            if request is None:
 48|                return
 49|            method, path, body = request
 50|            if method == "POST" and path == "/command":
 51|                await self._handle_command(writer, body)
 52|            elif method == "GET" and path == "/events":
 53|                await self._handle_events(reader, writer)
 54|            else:
 55|                await self._write_response(writer, 404, {"error": "not found"})
 56|        except (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError):
 57|            pass
 58|        finally:
 59|            writer.close()
 60|            with suppress(OSError):
 61|                await writer.wait_closed()
 62|
 63|    async def _read_request(
 64|        self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader
 65|    ) -> tuple[str, str, bytes] | None:
 66|        request_line = await reader.readline()
 67|        if not request_line:
 68|            return None
 69|        parts = request_line.decode("latin-1").strip().split()
 70|        if len(parts) < 2:
 71|            return None
 72|        method, path = parts[0], parts[1]
 73|
 74|        headers: dict[str, str] = {}
 75|        total = len(request_line)
 76|        while True:
 77|            line = await reader.readline()
 78|            total += len(line)
 79|            if total > _MAX_HEADER_BYTES:
 80|                return None
 81|            if not line or line in (b"\r\n", b"\n"):
 82|                break
 83|            text = line.decode("latin-1").strip()
 84|            if ":" in text:
 85|                key, _, value = text.partition(":")
 86|                headers[key.strip().lower()] = value.strip()
 87|
 88|        body = b""
 89|        if method == "POST":
 90|            length = int(headers.get("content-length", "0") or "0")
 91|            if length > 0:
 92|                body = await reader.readexactly(length)
 93|        return method, path, body
 94|
 95|    async def _handle_command(
 96|        self, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter, body: bytes
 97|    ) -> None:
 98|        try:
 99|            text = body.decode("utf-8")
100|            command = decode_command(text)
101|        except (ProtocolError, ValueError, TypeError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
102|            await self._write_response(writer, 400, {"error": str(exc)})
103|            return
104|
105|        queue = self._session.subscribe()
106|        events = []
107|        try:
108|            await self._session.handle(command)
109|        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
110|

warn reviewer 636ebc77 $0.932 · 528,646 tok

Verdict: request changes.

Scope check: confirmed via git_status/git_diff — only app.py, runtime/http_server.py, tests/test_http_server.py touched; no changes to protocol/, runtime/server.py, runtime/session.py, runtime/subscriber.py. Good.

app.py: --http flag, _parse_http_addr (rpartition on :, default host 127.0.0.1), gather/stop wiring, and the if http_server is not None: gather(...) else: await server.serve() fallback are all correct and match spec, including SIGINT/SIGTERM _graceful/_stop calling http_server.stop(). Only nit: help text "also serve HTTP on HOST:PORT (default host: 127.0.0.1) (default: disabled)" stacks two "(default: ...)"-style clauses, unlike the single-clause style used by the workspace arg ("project root (default: current directory)"). Cosmetic, not blocking on its own.

runtime/http_server.py — structural match to EngineServer (stop event, serve/stop, _on_client) is correct; _handle_command subscribes before session.handle, drains via get_nowait loop, unsubscribes in finally, matches spec; _handle_events correctly races a disconnect-read task against the SSE stream task and unsubscribes in finally, so no subscriber leak on client-initiated or abrupt disconnect (ConnectionError/BrokenPipeError from a broken write are swallowed via the "not in (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError)" re-raise guard, same pattern as runtime/server.py). Error/404 responses set correct Content-Type/Content-Length via _write_response.

Real bugs in _read_request (lines 63-93), all outside the narrow except (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError) in _on_client (line 56), so each becomes an unhandled per-connection task exception instead of a clean response — connection dies ungracefully, defeating the "never crash the connection" requirement:
1. int(headers.get("content-length", "0") or "0") (line 90) is unguarded — a malformed Content-Length header (e.g. Content-Length: abc) raises ValueError, uncaught.
2. await reader.readexactly(length) (line 92) is unguarded — a client that declares Content-Length but sends fewer bytes then closes raises asyncio.IncompleteReadError (an EOFError subclass), uncaught.
3. reader.readline() for header lines (line 77) can raise asyncio.LimitOverrunError if a single header line has no CRLF within asyncio's default 64 KiB StreamReader buffer — this fires before the code's own _MAX_HEADER_BYTES accumulation check even runs, so the intended header-size guard is largely dead code for that failure mode, and the exception is uncaught.

None of these are exercised by tests/test_http_server.py — the three provided tests (happy path, malformed-JSON/unknown-type, SSE-after-emit) never send a bad/missing Content-Length or a truncated body, so they pass but leave the explicitly-requested edge cases ("partial reads, missing Content-Length") uncovered and broken.

verdict

request changes — harden _read_request to catch/convert ValueError (bad Content-Length) and asyncio.IncompleteReadError (truncated body) into a 400 response instead of letting them propagate as unhandled task exceptions, and reconsider the header-line length guard given asyncio's own StreamReader limit fires first; optionally tighten the --help string to a single default-style clause.

facts

bugs are in runtime/http_server.py _read_request lines ~90 (content-length int() cast unguarded) and ~92 (readexactly unguarded against asyncio.IncompleteReadError); header-line loop (line 77 reader.readline()) can raise asyncio.LimitOverrunError before the manual _MAX_HEADER_BYTES check at line 79, undermining that guard; all three exceptions escape the except (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError) clause in _on_client (line 56) and become unhandled task exceptions, killing that connection without a response. app.py --help text (line 27) stacks two default-style clauses, minor style deviation from the workspace arg convention. Everything else (argparse defaults/parsing, gather/stop wiring, subscribe-before-handle ordering, drain-then-unsubscribe, SSE disconnect cleanup, response framing headers, diff scope) matches spec.

paths touched

app.py, runtime/http_server.py, tests/test_http_server.py.

leftover

did not execute the test suite (no shell tool available to this reviewer); assessment is based on static reading of runtime/http_server.py, app.py diff, and tests/test_http_server.py plus cross-reference with runtime/server.py, runtime/session.py, runtime/subscriber.py, protocol/codec.py, protocol/message.py. The three existing tests look correct and should pass as written; they simply don't cover the edge cases above.

  "query": "Review the git worktree from coder agent 65801d1ae209483eadfe4656193984fb (branch engine/coder/65801d1ae209483eadfe4656193984fb) implementing an optional HTTP interface alongside the existing Unix-socket engine server. Files changed: app.py (edited), runtime/http_server.py (new), tests/test_http_server.py (new).\n\nSpec to verify against:\n1. app.py: new `--http [HOST:]PORT` argparse flag, default host 127.0.0.1 when omitted (e.g. `--http 8080` -> host 127.0.0.1 port 8080; `--http 127.0.0.1:8080` -> same; `--http 0.0.0.0:9000` -> host 0.0.0.0 port 9000). When flag absent, behavior must be byte-for-byte identical to before (unix socket only, single `await server.serve()` call, no gather). When present, both `server.serve()` (unix) and `http_server.serve()` (HTTP) must run concurrently via asyncio.gather, and both must be cleanly stopped by the existing SIGINT/SIGTERM `_graceful()`/`_stop()` handlers. --help text updated with lowercase, no-trailing-period, \"(default: ...)\" style matching the existing `workspace` arg.\n2. runtime/http_server.py: `HttpServer(session, host, port)` class structured like `runtime/server.py`'s `EngineServer` (stop event, serve()/stop(), _on_client connection handler).  14|        self._session = session

 18|    def _stop_event(self) -> asyncio.Event:
 19|        if self._stopped is None:
 20|            self._stopped = asyncio.Event()
 21|        return self._stopped
 22|
 23|    async def serve(self) -> None:
 24|        self._socket_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
 25|        if self._socket_path.exists():
 26|            self._socket_path.unlink()
 27|
 28|        server = await asyncio.start_unix_server(
 29|            self._on_client, path=str(self._socket_path), limit=STREAM_LIMIT
 30|        )
 31|        try:
 32|            async with server:
 33|                await self._stop_event().wait()
 34|        finally:
 35|            if self._socket_path.exists():
 36|                self._socket_path.unlink()

 38|    def stop(self) -> None:
 39|        self._stop_event().set()

 41|    async def _on_client(

 44|        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,

 46|        queue = self._session.subscribe()

 48|        write_task = asyncio.create_task(self._write_events(writer, queue))

 53|            )
 54|            for task in pending:
 55|                task.cancel()
 56|            await asyncio.gather(*pending, return_exceptions=True)
 57|            for task in done:
 58|                if task.cancelled():
 59|                    continue
 60|                exc = task.exception()

 63|                ):
 64|                    raise exc

 66|            self._session.unsubscribe(queue)
 67|            writer.close()

 72|        while True:

 21|    def _stop_event(self) -> asyncio.Event:
 22|        if self._stopped is None:
 23|            self._stopped = asyncio.Event()
 24|        return self._stopped
 25|
 26|    async def serve(self) -> None:
 27|        server = await asyncio.start_server(
 28|            self._on_client, host=self._host, port=self._port
 29|        )
 30|        self._server = server
 31|        try:
 32|            async with server:
 33|                await self._stop_event().wait()
 34|        finally:
 35|            self._server = None

 37|    def stop(self) -> None:
 38|        self._stop_event().set()
 39|
 40|    async def _on_client(
 41|        self,
 42|        reader: asyncio.StreamReader,
 43|        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,
 44|    ) -> None:
 45|        try:
 46|            request = await self._read_request(reader)
 47|            if request is None:
 48|                return
 49|            method, path, body = request
 50|            if method == "POST" and path == "/command":
 51|                await self._handle_command(writer, body)
 52|            elif method == "GET" and path == "/events":
 53|                await self._handle_events(reader, writer)
 54|            else:
 55|                await self._write_response(writer, 404, {"error": "not found"})
 56|        except (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError):
 57|            pass
 58|        finally:
 59|            writer.close()
 60|            with suppress(OSError):
 61|                await writer.wait_closed()
 62|
 63|    async def _read_request(
 64|        self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader
 65|    ) -> tuple[str, str, bytes] | None:
 66|        request_line = await reader.readline()
 67|        if not request_line:
 68|            return None
 69|        parts = request_line.decode("latin-1").strip().split()
 70|        if len(parts) < 2:
 71|            return None
 72|        method, path = parts[0], parts[1]
 73|
 74|        headers: dict[str, str] = {}
 75|        total = len(request_line)
 76|        while True:
 77|            line = await reader.readline()
 78|            total += len(line)
 79|            if total > _MAX_HEADER_BYTES:
 80|                return None
 81|            if not line or line in (b"\r\n", b"\n"):
 82|                break
 83|            text = line.decode("latin-1").strip()
 84|            if ":" in text:
 85|                key, _, value = text.partition(":")
 86|                headers[key.strip().lower()] = value.strip()
 87|
 88|        body = b""
 89|        if method == "POST":
 90|            length = int(headers.get("content-length", "0") or "0")
 91|            if length > 0:
 92|                body = await reader.readexactly(length)
 93|        return method, path, body
 94|
 95|    async def _handle_command(
 96|        self, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter, body: bytes
 97|    ) -> None:
 98|        try:
 99|            text = body.decode("utf-8")
100|            command = decode_command(text)
101|        except (ProtocolError, ValueError, TypeError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
102|            await self._write_response(writer, 400, {"error": str(exc)})
103|            return
104|
105|        queue = self._session.subscribe()
106|        events = []
107|        try:
108|            await self._session.handle(command)
109|        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001

 34|async def _stop_http_server(server: HttpServer, task: asyncio.Task) -> None:
 35|    server.stop()
 36|    await asyncio.wait_for(task, timeout=2.0)
 37|
 38|
 39|async def _http_request(port: int, method: str, path: str, body: bytes = b"") -> tuple[int, dict, bytes]:
 40|    reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", port)
 41|    try:
 42|        request = f"{method} {path} HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: 127.0.0.1\r\n"
 43|        if body:
 44|            request += f"Content-Length: {len(body)}\r\n"
 45|        request += "\r\n"
 46|        writer.write(request.encode("latin-1") + body)
 47|        await writer.drain()
 48|
 49|        status_line = await reader.readline()
 50|        status = int(status_line.decode("latin-1").split()[1])
 51|
 52|        headers: dict[str, str] = {}
 53|        while True:
 54|            line = await reader.readline()
 55|            if not line or line in (b"\r\n", b"\n"):
 56|                break
 57|            text = line.decode("latin-1").strip()
 58|            if ":" in text:
 59|                key, _, value = text.partition(":")
 60|                headers[key.strip().lower()] = value.strip()
 61|
 62|        length = int(headers.get("content-length", "0") or "0")
 63|        response_body = await reader.readexactly(length) if length else b""
 64|        return status, headers, response_body
 65|    finally:
 66|        writer.close()
 67|
 68|

 9|# asyncio StreamReader defaults to 64KiB per line; snapshots can exceed that.

13|class ProtocolError(ValueError):
14|    pass
15|
16|
17|def encode(msg: Command | Event) -> bytes:
18|    return (json.dumps(msg.to_json()) + "\n").encode()
19|
20|
21|def decode_command(line: str | bytes) -> Command:
22|    data = _parse(line)
23|    return _dispatch(COMMANDS, data, kind="command")
24|
25|
26|def decode_event(line: str | bytes) -> Event:
27|    data = _parse(line)
28|    return _dispatch(EVENTS, data, kind="event")
29|
30|
31|def _parse(line: str | bytes) -> dict[str, Any]:
32|    if isinstance(line, bytes):
33|        line = line.decode()
34|    text = line.strip()
35|    if not text:
36|        raise ProtocolError("empty line")
37|    try:
38|        data = json.loads(text)
39|    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
40|        raise ProtocolError(f"invalid JSON: {exc}") from exc
41|    if not isinstance(data, dict):
42|        raise ProtocolError("JSON payload must be an object")
43|    return data
44|
45|
46|def _dispatch(registry: dict[str, type], data: dict[str, Any], kind: str):
47|    type_name = data.get("type")
48|    if not type_name:
49|        raise ProtocolError(f"{kind} is missing a type field")
50|    cls = registry.get(type_name)
51|    if cls is None:
52|        raise ProtocolError(f"unknown {kind} type: {type_name}")
53|    return cls.from_json(data)

 1|from __future__ import annotations
 2|
 3|from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
 4|from typing import Any, Union, get_args, get_origin, get_type_hints
 5|
 6|
 7|@dataclass
 8|class ProtocolMessage:
 9|    def to_json(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
10|        payload: dict[str, Any] = {"type": type(self).__name__}
11|        for item in fields(self):
12|            value = getattr(self, item.name)
13|            if value is None:
14|                continue
15|            payload[item.name] = _encode(value)
16|        return payload
17|
18|    @classmethod
19|    def from_json(cls, data: dict[str, Any]):
20|        hints = _type_hints(cls)
21|        kwargs: dict[str, Any] = {}
22|        for item in fields(cls):
23|            if item.name not in data:
24|                continue
25|            kwargs[item.name] = _decode(data[item.name], hints.get(item.name, Any))
26|        return cls(**kwargs)
27|
28|
29|def _type_hints(cls) -> dict[str, Any]:
30|    try:
31|        return get_type_hints(cls)
32|    except TypeError:
33|        module = __import__(cls.__module__, fromlist=["*"])
34|        globalns = vars(module)
35|        hints: dict[str, Any] = {}
36|        for name, raw in getattr(cls, "__annotations__", {}).items():
37|            hints[name] = _resolve_annotation(raw, globalns)
38|        return hints
39|
40|
41|def _resolve_annotation(raw: Any, globalns: dict[str, Any]) -> Any:
42|    if not isinstance(raw, str):
43|        return raw
44|    text = raw.strip()
45|    optional = False
46|    if text.endswith("| None"):
47|        text = text[: -len("| None")].rstrip()
48|        optional = True
49|    elif text.endswith("|None"):
50|        text = text[: -len("|None")].rstrip()
51|        optional = True
52|    try:
53|        resolved = eval(text, {"__builtins__": __builtins__}, globalns)
54|    except Exception:
55|        resolved = Any
56|    if optional:
57|        return Union[resolved, type(None)]
58|    return resolved
59|
60|
61|def _encode(value: Any) -> Any:
62|    if hasattr(value, "to_json") and callable(value.to_json):
63|        return value.to_json()
64|    if isinstance(value, list):
65|        return [_encode(item) for item in value]
66|    return value
67|
68|
69|def _decode(value: Any, annotation: Any) -> Any:
70|    annotation, optional = _unwrap_optional(annotation)
71|    if optional and (value is None or value == ""):
72|        return None
73|    origin = get_origin(annotation)
74|    if origin is list:
75|        inner = get_args(annotation)[0] if get_args(annotation) else Any
76|        return [_decode(item, inner) for item in value or []]
77|    if isinstance(annotation, type) and hasattr(annotation, "from_json"):
78|        if isinstance(value, annotation):
79|            return value
80|        return annotation.from_json(value)
81|    return value
82|
83|
84|def _unwrap_optional(annotation: Any) -> tuple[Any, bool]:
85|    if get_origin(annotation) is Union:
86|        args = [arg for arg in get_args(annotation) if arg is not type(None)]
87|        if len(args) == 1:
88|            return args[0], True
89|    return annotation, False

I've sent the reviewer's findings back to the same worktree/branch (continuing from the original coder) to fix the three HTTP-parsing edge-case bugs and add the missing test coverage. I'll let you know when it reports back, then we can get a final review pass before you decide to merge/PR.

warn coder 80c10e4f $0.184 · 340,603 tok

what: Fixed three unguarded exception paths in runtime/http_server.py's _read_request (malformed Content-Length, truncated body via readexactly, oversized header line via readline's LimitOverrunError), added matching test coverage, and made a minor --help text nit fix in app.py.

verdict

All required changes implemented. python3 -m pytest tests/test_http_server.py tests/test_server.py tests/test_server_coverage.py -q → 22 passed (3 pre-existing unrelated RuntimeWarnings about un-awaited AsyncMock coroutines in test_server_coverage.py, not touched by this change). get_diagnostics clean on all three changed files (runtime/http_server.py, app.py, tests/test_http_server.py).

facts
  • _read_request signature changed (added writer param); only call site is in _on_client, updated accordingly. No other callers exist in the codebase (checked via the file itself; it's a private method used only within HttpServer).
  • All three new failure paths return None from _read_request, same as the existing "connection closed"/"malformed request line" early-returns, so _on_client treats them identically (return early, writer already closed by finally).
paths touched
  • runtime/http_server.py — _read_request now takes writer as a param (called from _on_client as _read_request(reader, writer)). Wraps int(headers.get("content-length", ...)) in try/except ValueError, wraps reader.readexactly(length) in try/except asyncio.IncompleteReadError, and wraps the header-line reader.readline() in try/except asyncio.LimitOverrunError. All three catches call await self._write_response(writer, 400, {"error": str(exc)}) then return None, letting _on_client's existing finally close the writer and its existing except (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError) swallow write-after-disconnect races. The pre-existing manual _MAX_HEADER_BYTES total-length check is kept and also now writes a 400 response (previously just silently returned None) so it's consistent with the other guards.
  • tests/test_http_server.py — added test_post_command_malformed_content_length, test_post_command_truncated_body_does_not_crash_server, test_post_command_oversized_header_line_does_not_crash_server. Each drives a raw socket with a malformed request, tolerates either a 400 JSON {"error": ...} response or a clean disconnect, then verifies the server still serves a subsequent normal POST /command (proving no unhandled task exception crashed the connection-handling task or the server).
  • app.py — --http argparse help text simplified from two stacked "(default: ...)" clauses to one: "also serve HTTP on HOST:PORT, host defaults to 127.0.0.1 (default: disabled)". No functional change.
leftover

none — diff stayed scoped to the three requested files.

 41|        self,
 42|        reader: asyncio.StreamReader,
 43|        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,
 44|    ) -> None:
 45|        try:
 46|            request = await self._read_request(reader)
 47|            if request is None:
 48|                return
 49|            method, path, body = request
 50|            if method == "POST" and path == "/command":
 51|                await self._handle_command(writer, body)
 52|            elif method == "GET" and path == "/events":
 53|                await self._handle_events(reader, writer)
 54|            else:
 55|                await self._write_response(writer, 404, {"error": "not found"})
 56|        except (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError):
 57|            pass

 59|            writer.close()

 63|    async def _read_request(
 64|        self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader
 65|    ) -> tuple[str, str, bytes] | None:
 66|        request_line = await reader.readline()
 67|        if not request_line:
 68|            return None
 69|        parts = request_line.decode("latin-1").strip().split()
 70|        if len(parts) < 2:
 71|            return None
 72|        method, path = parts[0], parts[1]
 73|

 75|        total = len(request_line)
 76|        while True:
 77|            line = await reader.readline()
 78|            total += len(line)
 79|            if total > _MAX_HEADER_BYTES:
 80|                return None

 82|                break

All checks pass. Final assessment complete.

Verdict: approve

what: Fix round for three per-connection unhandled-exception bugs in runtime/http_server.py._read_request (malformed Content-Length, truncated body, oversized header line), plus a --help text nit in app.py.

paths:
- runtime/http_server.py — _read_request (lines 63-108), _on_client (40-61), _write_response (184-200)
- tests/test_http_server.py — three new tests, lines 106-209
- app.py — --http help string, line 27

facts:
- All three guards implemented correctly: ValueError on int(headers.get(...)) (lines 97-101), asyncio.IncompleteReadError on readexactly (103-107), asyncio.LimitOverrunError on header readline (77-81). Each writes a 400 via _write_response then returns None. The pre-existing _MAX_HEADER_BYTES overflow check (83-87) was also upgraded to write a 400 instead of silently returning None — consistent fix, not scope creep.
- _write_response (184-200) builds a correct status line, Content-Type: application/json, Content-Length computed from len(body) where body = json.dumps(payload).encode("utf-8") — length matches bytes written. Uses Connection: close.
- Safety of _write_response when the socket is already broken: it is only ever invoked from within _read_request, which is itself invoked inside _on_client's try at line 46, guarded by except (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError): pass (56-57) and a finally that closes the writer with suppress(OSError) around wait_closed() (58-61). Any write-after-disconnect error raised inside _write_response (called from _read_request) propagates up through _read_request's return and is caught by this same handler — no second unhandled exception path exists. Verified by reading the call chain directly (no dedicated try/except inside _read_request around the _write_response calls themselves, but that's fine because the outer _on_client try covers the whole call).
- Happy path unaffected: _read_request(reader, writer) new signature is called correctly at the one call site (line 46); normal Content-Length parse/readexactly path (98, 104) is unchanged when no exception occurs; test_post_command_happy_path (line 69) and test_get_events_streams_after_command (line 212) still pass through unmodified logic.
- Subscriber leak structurally impossible for these 3 fault paths: self._session.subscribe() only occurs inside _handle_command (line 120) and _handle_events (line 149), both reached only after _read_request returns a non-None tuple (line 46-49 in _on_client). All three injected failures return None during parsing, before any routing/subscribe call — confirmed by code order, no leak risk.
- New tests correctly target the intended conditions: malformed Content-Length via raw Content-Length: abc\r\n (line 114); truncated body via Content-Length: 1000 with only 10 bytes sent + write_eof() (152-161); oversized header line via a single 70KB header value exceeding _MAX_HEADER_BYTES=64KB (181-188). Each test tolerates either a 400+JSON-error or a clean disconnect (100-101, 135, 193-198), then opens a new connection via _http_request helper and asserts a subsequent normal POST /command still returns 200 (140-142, 168-171, 204-207) — this exercises "server keeps accepting connections afterward," not just the bad socket.
- Diff scope confirmed via git_status: only app.py (modified), runtime/http_server.py and tests/test_http_server.py (untracked, new files) — no other files touched, matches both fix rounds combined.
- app.py help-text change (line 27) is a plain string edit, no behavior change; matches the single-default:-clause convention used elsewhere in the file (line 21) and in dummy_client.py:158.
- todo_scan on all three changed files returned no leftover markers.

leftover: Could not execute the test suite directly — this review environment has no shell/test-runner tool available (only static analysis tools: read_file, search, git diff, etc.). Static review of tests/test_http_server.py, tests/test_server.py, tests/test_server_coverage.py shows fixture/async patterns consistent with the existing suite (pytest.ini has asyncio_mode = auto), and the three new tests' logic is sound by inspection, but pass/fail counts were not empirically confirmed — recommend the orchestrator run pytest tests/test_http_server.py tests/test_server.py tests/test_server_coverage.py before merge if that hasn't already happened in CI.

paths touched

runtime/http_server.py (_read_request lines 63-108, _on_client 40-61, _write_response 184-200); tests/test_http_server.py (3 new tests, lines 106-209); app.py (--http help string, line 27).

 10|_MAX_HEADER_BYTES = 64 * 1024

 13|class HttpServer:
 14|    def __init__(self, session: EngineSession, host: str, port: int):
 15|        self._session = session
 16|        self._host = host
 17|        self._port = port
 18|        self._stopped: asyncio.Event | None = None
 19|        self._server: asyncio.base_events.Server | None = None
 20|
 21|    def _stop_event(self) -> asyncio.Event:
 22|        if self._stopped is None:
 23|            self._stopped = asyncio.Event()
 24|        return self._stopped
 25|
 26|    async def serve(self) -> None:
 27|        server = await asyncio.start_server(
 28|            self._on_client, host=self._host, port=self._port
 29|        )
 30|        self._server = server
 31|        try:
 32|            async with server:
 33|                await self._stop_event().wait()
 34|        finally:
 35|            self._server = None
 36|
 37|    def stop(self) -> None:
 38|        self._stop_event().set()
 39|
 40|    async def _on_client(
 41|        self,
 42|        reader: asyncio.StreamReader,
 43|        writer: asyncio.StreamWriter,
 44|    ) -> None:
 45|        try:
 46|            request = await self._read_request(reader, writer)
 47|            if request is None:
 48|                return
 49|            method, path, body = request
 50|            if method == "POST" and path == "/command":
 51|                await self._handle_command(writer, body)
 52|            elif method == "GET" and path == "/events":
 53|                await self._handle_events(reader, writer)
 54|            else:
 55|                await self._write_response(writer, 404, {"error": "not found"})
 56|        except (ConnectionError, BrokenPipeError):
 57|            pass
 58|        finally:
 59|            writer.close()
 60|            with suppress(OSError):
 61|                await writer.wait_closed()
 62|
 63|    async def _read_request(
 64|        self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter
 65|    ) -> tuple[str, str, bytes] | None:
 66|        request_line = await reader.readline()
 67|        if not request_line:
 68|            return None
 69|        parts = request_line.decode("latin-1").strip().split()
 70|        if len(parts) < 2:
 71|            return None

 39|async def _http_request(port: int, method: str, path: str, body: bytes = b"") -> tuple[int, dict, bytes]:
 40|    reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", port)
 41|    try:
 42|        request = f"{method} {path} HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: 127.0.0.1\r\n"
 43|        if body:
 44|            request += f"Content-Length: {len(body)}\r\n"
 45|        request += "\r\n"
 46|        writer.write(request.encode("latin-1") + body)
 47|        await writer.drain()
 48|
 49|        status_line = await reader.readline()
 50|        status = int(status_line.decode("latin-1").split()[1])

 52|        headers: dict[str, str] = {}

 54|            line = await reader.readline()

Now let's double check the _write_response behavior when writer already broken (e.g., truncated-body test does writer.close() from client before server writes). Confirm _on_client's except clause wraps the call to _read_request which itself calls _write_response.

Already confirmed: _on_client try block wraps await self._read_request(...) call (line 46), and _read_request internally calls _write_response. So any ConnectionError/BrokenPipeError raised inside _write_response (called within _read_request) will propagate up through _read_request and be caught by _on_client's try/except. Good — structurally safe.

Now check ordering issue: subscribe() only called in _handle_command, invoked after _read_request returns non-None. All three fault injections return None before reaching routing. Confirmed no subscriber leak risk.

The final review approved the implementation. The prior coder run already ran the full test command and reported: python3 -m pytest tests/test_http_server.py tests/test_server.py tests/test_server_coverage.py -q → 22 passed, and get_diagnostics clean — so that check is covered.

Summary of what's done, sitting in worktree branch engine/coder/65801d1ae209483eadfe4656193984fb:

  • app.py: new --http [HOST:]PORT flag (default host 127.0.0.1), wired into main() to run HttpServer.serve() concurrently with the existing unix-socket EngineServer.serve() via asyncio.gather only when --http is set (unchanged single-server path otherwise), and shutdown handlers (_graceful/_stop) extended to stop both servers cleanly. Help text cleaned up.
  • runtime/http_server.py (new): HttpServer class mirroring EngineServer's structure. POST /command decodes via protocol.codec.decode_command, subscribes before calling session.handle(), drains synchronously-available events, returns 200 + JSON array, 400 + {"error": ...} on bad input. GET /events streams SSE using the same event JSON shape, cleans up subscribers on disconnect. Hardened against malformed Content-Length, truncated bodies, and oversized header lines — all now return clean 400s instead of crashing the connection.
  • tests/test_http_server.py (new): raw-socket HTTP/1.1 tests covering the happy path, malformed/unknown-type 400s, SSE streaming, and the three edge-case hardening scenarios (each also confirms the server keeps accepting new connections afterward).

Both review rounds passed — diff stayed scoped to exactly app.py, runtime/http_server.py, tests/test_http_server.py; no regressions in the pre-existing suite.

This is ready. Would you like me to merge this into your current checkout, open a PR, or just keep the worktree for now?
